## 1. Status values

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `PENDING` | Not started. Free to claim **only if** its phase is UNLOCKED and its dependencies are DONE. |
| `IN_PROGRESS` | Claimed by one agent (Agent + Updated columns filled). Nobody else touches it. |
| `REVIEW` | Work finished, waiting for owner review or approval. |
| `DONE` | Accepted: the phase document's acceptance criteria for this task are met, local tests pass, and the owner approved. |
| `BLOCKED` | Waiting for an owner decision, CA input, or another task. The reason is in Notes. |
| `DEFERRED` | Moved to a later phase by an owner decision (the target is in Notes). Not claimable in its original phase. |

**Version-control status (CD-67, 2026-10-13):** the status values above describe the *task implementation status* only (implemented, tested, independently reviewed, owner-approved). Separately, the *version-control status* of every task is **uncommitted**: no code or file is committed at any intermediate stage of any phase; the owner commits service-wise only after all planned phases are complete. `DONE` never means "committed", and a missing intermediate commit is never a reason to stop development. Agents run no SVN command that changes repository or working-copy state.

"Existing code: …" in Notes means part of the behaviour already exists in today's code (`PARTIALLY_COMPLETED` in the phase document). The task is still `PENDING` until it meets the target design.

---

## 2. How to use this file (every agent, every session)

1. **Read** AGENT_HANDOFF.md, then this file, then WORK_LOG.md.
2. **Check the gate:** implementation tasks may start only when Stage 0 item S0-1 (architecture approval) is DONE and the task's phase is `UNLOCKED` in §4.
3. **Pick** a `PENDING` task whose dependencies are DONE, inside the task area the owner gave you.
4. **Claim it before working:** set `IN_PROGRESS`, your agent/account name and the date. Save the file. One agent works on one task at a time.
5. **Plan first:** write the plan in chat and wait for the owner's approval before changing code.
6. **Finish:** set `REVIEW` (or `DONE` after owner approval), add a short note (what changed, tests run), and append a WORK_LOG.md entry.
7. **Never** change another agent's `IN_PROGRESS` row. If something is unclear, stop and ask the owner.
8. When all tasks of a phase are DONE, the phase is set to `DONE` in §4 — and the next phase to `UNLOCKED` if its dependencies are DONE — **only after an explicit owner decision** (CD-43 item 7b, 2026-10-12; precedent CD-33).

Non-blocking observations / follow-ups retained, not fixed in T6 (no implementation authorized): (F-1) TX-12 concurrency: `TaxRuleService.create` reads the first version without a lock and `update` locks only its own row — an identity-changing update of a code's only DRAFT version and the simultaneous creation of its version 2 can both succeed (versions of one code with different identities); `approve` does not re-check the identity against the first version; not covered by the concurrency ITs — follow-up candidate (lock the code's versions in create / update, or re-check at approval); (F-2) `SAMPLE-*` rows can be updated / approved through the API; once APPROVED they count as real configuration (gate passes, `sample = false`) — no TX rule covers it, a guard needs a new owner decision; (F-3) TX-3 × TX-1: retiring a future APPROVED successor leaves the superseded predecessor ending on D − 1 (TX-1 forbids reopening it) — a coverage gap from D until a new version is approved (production 409 for those dates); (F-4) the TX-13 literal reading is global: any APPROVED tax rule valid on the date disables the SAMPLE rules for that date for every supply type (e.g. dev with only an APPROVED INTRA rule: INTER / EXPORT / SEZ answer 409, never SAMPLE); per-supply-type behaviour would need a new decision; (F-5) TX-6 literal: SEZ and OVERSEAS are B2C (only REGISTERED_* are B2B) although SEZ units are normally GST-registered — revisit if needed; (F-6) the TX-9 gate counts any APPROVED INTRA / INTER rule incl. SAC- or plan-specific ones, so a generic resolution can still answer 409 while the gate passes — as decided; (F-7) test gaps: no resolver test of the seller supersede boundary D − 1 / D (the same query is proven for tax rules); the concurrency ITs assert the final state, not lock contention; the startup WARN text is not asserted; "no SAMPLE rows on a normal startup" is proven only by the `@Profile("sample")` test; (F-8) environment, unrelated to T6: ACCOUNT-BOOK unit tests write 6 files per reactor run into the unversioned `ACCOUNT-BOOK-SERVICE/uploads/` (since 2026-10-02) — left unchanged. Risks: residual (non-blocking, owner to note; corrected 2026-10-10 in the final correction round): (R1) the backlog is in memory per instance — after a restart an evicted company stays denied (fail closed, availability only) until its next event or the internal refresh endpoint; the re-sync scheduler is OFF by default (`erp.module-entitlement.sync.enabled=false`) and is not an automatic recovery mechanism unless explicitly enabled; (R2) Redis unavailable while evicting → the old key lives until its TTL (`MODULE_ENTITLEMENT_TTL`, 300 s); the same holds for a committed change whose after-commit publish fails (`CompanyEntitlementSyncListener` logs it, nothing is listed); a later retry of a listed company re-publishes (a failed publication keeps it listed, N3); if `expire` failed after `add`, the key would have no TTL (see R4); (R3) resolved for the known paths by the final round (N1): subscription operations lock the company right after their subscription, manual grants lock it first, out-of-transaction emitters recompute in their own transaction — their reads are taken after the company lock; any future emitter must keep this order; (R4) N4 / pre-existing, kept for P06-T6: the publish (delete / add / expire) is not atomic and concurrent publishes are not ordered; a retry that publishes while another committed change of the company fails its recompute can leave that change's previous rows in the key until the next retry (the renewed entry stays listed) or the TTL; (R5) a permanently failing recompute (data conflict) is audited once per outage (`ENTITLEMENT_RECOMPUTE_FAILED`, plus one `ENTITLEMENT_RECOMPUTE_REJECTED` per triggering change, none per retry) and retried with a doubling delay from 30 s up to 1 h (`recovery-retry-interval-ms`, `recovery-max-retry-interval-ms`), each failed retry logged at WARN with its attempt number — until the data is fixed; (R6) unchanged review notes (N6): a `DEP:` row is keyed and `source_item_id`-attributed to the lowest-id reaching parent item while its validity is the union of all reaching parents (access correct, attribution partial); an INCLUDED target reached through a module provided by a DEPENDENCY item takes its validity from the plan item at the start of the chain; descendants of a missing CHARGED dependency; the item-status deny-list; `ModuleEntitlementServiceTest` kept until P06-T8; test gaps: the IST / UTC boundary and inclusive-date tests characterise behaviour that was already correct (they would also pass on the code before the corrections — they prove no fix), no web-layer test of the 409 `MANUAL_GRANT_NOT_ALLOWED`, no MySQL IT for a RETIRED module or a commit-time grant failure, the commit-time grant audit was not mutation-tested, the IT's Lettuce factory is not destroyed. Earlier: **REVIEW 2026-10-10 — correction round complete; owner review pending (never DONE by the agent).** Corrections of review findings 1–7: (1) A4-7 corrected: company lifecycle transitions, `PlatformSubscriptionService.correctDates` and provisioning emit outside a transaction; there a failed recompute no longer fails the committed change — `EntitlementRecomputeListener` hands it to the new `EntitlementRecomputeRecovery` (company listed in the in-memory `EntitlementRecomputeBacklog`, `COMPANY_MODULES` key evicted at once = fail closed, FAILED audit `ENTITLEMENT_RECOMPUTE_FAILED` once per outage, scheduled retry `retryPending` every `erp.module-entitlement.recovery-retry-interval-ms` (30 s) = recompute in its own transaction + publish, SUCCESS audit `ENTITLEMENT_RECOMPUTE_RECOVERED`); while listed, `publish` keeps the key evicted (stale rows are never published) and a successful `refresh` (also the internal refresh endpoint) removes it; inside a transaction a failure still rolls the change back (A4-1 / A4-2); (2) diff-apply plans every change first and changes the rows releasing a charged guard, flushes them, then changes / flushes the other rows, then inserts (a revived lower-id charged row no longer hits `uk_company_entitlement_charged_guard`); (3) `EntitlementEngine.lockCompany` re-reads the company under `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE` (`EntityManager.refresh`) — used by the recompute and the manual grant; (4) `DEP:` rows are valid for the union of the reaching parent items' validity (item dates ∩ PAID coverage, A4-4);
